  ~ /hazelcast/5.3/getting-started/get-started-cli
      [upstream] L1: - "+ .RPM"
          UPSTREAM MIS-RENDER that the golden faithfully reproduces, and which the converter DELIBERATELY REPAIRS -- so this is one of the few places our output is more correct than the golden. The source writes a stray `+` list-continuation immediately before the `.RPM` block title with no list open (install-hazelcast.adoc, and get-started-cli.adoc which includes the same region). Asciidoctor cannot attach the title to anything, so it emits both lines as one literal paragraph -- the 5.4 and 5.3 golden contain `<p>+\n.RPM</p>` and NO block title at all -- and the following code block loses its title. converter/lib/html-to-mdx fixSwallowedBlockTitles detects exactly that shape (a paragraph of `+` then `.Title`, immediately followed by an untitled listing/literal block), drops the stray paragraph and re-attaches the title, so our fence carries title="RPM" as the author intended. THIS HALF is the golden's literal `+ .RPM` paragraph, which we deliberately do not reproduce / our recovered `RPM` title, which the golden has no counterpart for. WHY ONLY 5.4 AND 5.3: upstream fixed the source at 5.5, where the same page renders `<div class="title">RPM</div>` correctly and the repair never fires -- which is why this needs no accept entry at any later version. A source fix is the real cure, but frozen versions cannot be re-sourced. Goes stale, and the gate says so, if these branches are ever re-sourced.
      [upstream] L1: + "RPM"
          UPSTREAM MIS-RENDER that the golden faithfully reproduces, and which the converter DELIBERATELY REPAIRS -- so this is one of the few places our output is more correct than the golden. The source writes a stray `+` list-continuation immediately before the `.RPM` block title with no list open (install-hazelcast.adoc, and get-started-cli.adoc which includes the same region). Asciidoctor cannot attach the title to anything, so it emits both lines as one literal paragraph -- the 5.4 and 5.3 golden contain `<p>+\n.RPM</p>` and NO block title at all -- and the following code block loses its title. converter/lib/html-to-mdx fixSwallowedBlockTitles detects exactly that shape (a paragraph of `+` then `.Title`, immediately followed by an untitled listing/literal block), drops the stray paragraph and re-attaches the title, so our fence carries title="RPM" as the author intended. THIS HALF is the golden's literal `+ .RPM` paragraph, which we deliberately do not reproduce / our recovered `RPM` title, which the golden has no counterpart for. WHY ONLY 5.4 AND 5.3: upstream fixed the source at 5.5, where the same page renders `<div class="title">RPM</div>` correctly and the repair never fires -- which is why this needs no accept entry at any later version. A source fix is the real cure, but frozen versions cannot be re-sourced. Goes stale, and the gate says so, if these branches are ever re-sourced.

      [harness-limit] L3: dead-link /assets/files/nasdaqlisted-b0da91cd767daa30b2410cebdf5bf247.txt
          HARNESS LIMIT, not a conversion defect: Layer 3 resolves a link path against the ANTORA MANIFEST, which records published Antora resources and knows nothing of webpack's output, so a content-hashed asset URL can never resolve there.

HOW THE LINK BECAME A HASHED ASSET. nasdaqlisted.txt is the corpus's ONLY Antora attachment (hz-docs docs/modules/pipelines/attachments/, 195 KB), a data file this tutorial tells the reader to download. Antora publishes it and the manifest records it as `attachment$nasdaqlisted.txt`, so the production URL /hazelcast/<seg>/pipelines/_attachments/nasdaqlisted.txt IS a resolvable manifest resource -- which is why this page was Layer-3 clean before. Attachments are a different resource family from images, so extract-images.js never placed the file and the converter has no attachment path: it was silently dropped and 404ing on all 6 versions. It is now placed under static/ at that production URL, and Docusaurus resolves an ABSOLUTE asset path against every staticDirs entry (mdx-loader lib/remark/transformLinks/index.js, getLocalFileAbsolutePath): finding the file, it rewrites the link to a webpack require, so the built page carries /assets/files/nasdaqlisted-<hash>.txt -- one shared asset for all 6 versions, since identical bytes hash identically.

WHY THIS IS SAFE TO ACCEPT. That URL can only exist as the result of a require that FAILS THE BUILD if the file is missing, so the link is verified more strictly than the manifest could verify it. Note the inversion being corrected here: while the file was missing, the page carried the production URL, the manifest resolved it, and Layer 3 was GREEN on a link that genuinely 404'd for every reader. It is now red on a link that works. Accepting the hashed URL is also consistent with the standing decision that co-located image URLs are not production-preserved (webpack hashes them) -- an attachment is the same class of asset, and co-locating this one instead of using static/ would produce the SAME hashed URL and the same six entries.

The static/ copies stay so Antora's production URL keeps serving for inbound links and bookmarks; the pages themselves use the hashed asset.

GOES STALE, and the gate says so, if: the link is switched to Docusaurus's `pathname://` prefix to opt out of asset processing (the production URL returns, and these entries are no longer matched); or a Docusaurus upgrade changes the content-hash format, which moves the hash in the key so the gate re-flags it and a human re-checks -- deny-by-default working as intended rather than a maintenance burden.

      [wont-fix] L1: - "Enterprise Enhanced the experience of using Hazelcast with persistence under Kubernetes by removing requirements for manual interventions. Previously, when there is a restart on such a cluster, the cluster states were needed to be changed manually. For some cases, this might have led to data loss. Now, the shutdown process is automated. See Configuring Kubernetes for details. #21844"
          DELIBERATE DIVERGENCE, decided by Miranda 2026-08-27 -- the same decision recorded on management-center/getting-started/overview, reached from the other side of it. The inline enterprise badge has ONE label site-wide (src/components/Badge), and hz-docs is internally inconsistent about what the badge says: most pages write the attribute -- [.enterprise]*{enterprise-product-name}* -- which resolves to "Enterprise Edition", while THIS page writes the literal [.enterprise]*Enterprise*. Since enterprise-product-name IS "Enterprise Edition", the literal is a source inconsistency, not a second value. The registry label is "Enterprise Edition", so the pages using the attribute are fidelity-clean and these literal ones diverge by exactly the two words the decision added.       [upstream] L3: dead-anchor-cross /hazelcast/5.4/maintain-cluster/rest-api#removing-entries-from-a-map-for-rest-client
          UPSTREAM CONTENT BUG in hz-docs, already recorded in orphancheck/allowlist.json under hazelcast:events/distributed-object-events. events/object-events deep-links to `clients:rest#removing-entries-from-a-map-for-rest-client`, and that anchor exists on NEITHER page: the clients/rest golden is a 9-line stub carrying no ids AT ALL, so the link is broken in the golden too -- it lands the reader at the top of a stub. The correct target is `#removing-entries-from-a-map`, which DOES exist on maintain-cluster/rest-api and which the stale fork events/distributed-object-events already links to correctly; the maintained page is the one that is wrong. WHY IT IS NOT SUBTRACTED AS GOLDEN BREAKAGE: clients/rest is dispositioned `redirect` -> maintain-cluster/rest-api, so the converter retargets the 7 inbound xrefs at conversion time and this link now resolves to a DIFFERENT path than the golden's. Layer 3's baseline identity is `dead-anchor-cross|{path}|{anchor}` (fidelity/lib/baseline.js problemKey), so moving the path changes the key and the known-broken golden entry no longer matches. The link is equally broken before and after -- the conversion introduced nothing -- but it now points at a better page. Accepted rather than fixed in the converter: repairing the fragment means encoding content knowledge about one specific link in migration tooling, and it would diverge from the golden's href either way, so it needs this entry regardless. UPSTREAM REPORT: change the xref on events/object-events.adoc to `xref:maintain-cluster:rest-api.adoc#removing-entries-from-a-map`. Fixing it upstream makes this entry go stale and the gate will say so.

  ~ /hazelcast/5.4/getting-started/get-started-cli
      [upstream] L1: - "+ .RPM"
          UPSTREAM MIS-RENDER that the golden faithfully reproduces, and which the converter DELIBERATELY REPAIRS -- so this is one of the few places our output is more correct than the golden. The source writes a stray `+` list-continuation immediately before the `.RPM` block title with no list open (install-hazelcast.adoc, and get-started-cli.adoc which includes the same region). Asciidoctor cannot attach the title to anything, so it emits both lines as one literal paragraph -- the 5.4 and 5.3 golden contain `<p>+\n.RPM</p>` and NO block title at all -- and the following code block loses its title. converter/lib/html-to-mdx fixSwallowedBlockTitles detects exactly that shape (a paragraph of `+` then `.Title`, immediately followed by an untitled listing/literal block), drops the stray paragraph and re-attaches the title, so our fence carries title="RPM" as the author intended. THIS HALF is the golden's literal `+ .RPM` paragraph, which we deliberately do not reproduce / our recovered `RPM` title, which the golden has no counterpart for. WHY ONLY 5.4 AND 5.3: upstream fixed the source at 5.5, where the same page renders `<div class="title">RPM</div>` correctly and the repair never fires -- which is why this needs no accept entry at any later version. A source fix is the real cure, but frozen versions cannot be re-sourced. Goes stale, and the gate says so, if these branches are ever re-sourced.

Text-only and reader-correct: the badge renders, and it names the edition the page means. wont-fix rather than a conversion fix -- matching the golden here would mean either baking a per-page label (defeating the one-registry design) or reverting the label decision. Goes stale, and the gate says so, if hz-docs switches this page to the attribute upstream.

  ~ /hazelcast/5.4/pipelines/map-join
      [harness-limit] L3: dead-link /assets/files/nasdaqlisted-b0da91cd767daa30b2410cebdf5bf247.txt
          HARNESS LIMIT, not a conversion defect: Layer 3 resolves a link path against the ANTORA MANIFEST, which records published Antora resources and knows nothing of webpack's output, so a content-hashed asset URL can never resolve there.

HOW THE LINK BECAME A HASHED ASSET. nasdaqlisted.txt is the corpus's ONLY Antora attachment (hz-docs docs/modules/pipelines/attachments/, 195 KB), a data file this tutorial tells the reader to download. Antora publishes it and the manifest records it as `attachment$nasdaqlisted.txt`, so the production URL /hazelcast/<seg>/pipelines/_attachments/nasdaqlisted.txt IS a resolvable manifest resource -- which is why this page was Layer-3 clean before. Attachments are a different resource family from images, so extract-images.js never placed the file and the converter has no attachment path: it was silently dropped and 404ing on all 6 versions. It is now placed under static/ at that production URL, and Docusaurus resolves an ABSOLUTE asset path against every staticDirs entry (mdx-loader lib/remark/transformLinks/index.js, getLocalFileAbsolutePath): finding the file, it rewrites the link to a webpack require, so the built page carries /assets/files/nasdaqlisted-<hash>.txt -- one shared asset for all 6 versions, since identical bytes hash identically.

WHY THIS IS SAFE TO ACCEPT. That URL can only exist as the result of a require that FAILS THE BUILD if the file is missing, so the link is verified more strictly than the manifest could verify it. Note the inversion being corrected here: while the file was missing, the page carried the production URL, the manifest resolved it, and Layer 3 was GREEN on a link that genuinely 404'd for every reader. It is now red on a link that works. Accepting the hashed URL is also consistent with the standing decision that co-located image URLs are not production-preserved (webpack hashes them) -- an attachment is the same class of asset, and co-locating this one instead of using static/ would produce the SAME hashed URL and the same six entries.

The static/ copies stay so Antora's production URL keeps serving for inbound links and bookmarks; the pages themselves use the hashed asset.

GOES STALE, and the gate says so, if: the link is switched to Docusaurus's `pathname://` prefix to opt out of asset processing (the production URL returns, and these entries are no longer matched); or a Docusaurus upgrade changes the content-hash format, which moves the hash in the key so the gate re-flags it and a human re-checks -- deny-by-default working as intended rather than a maintenance burden.

      [upstream] L3: dead-anchor-cross /hazelcast/5.5/maintain-cluster/rest-api#removing-entries-from-a-map-for-rest-client
          UPSTREAM CONTENT BUG in hz-docs, already recorded in orphancheck/allowlist.json under hazelcast:events/distributed-object-events. events/object-events deep-links to `clients:rest#removing-entries-from-a-map-for-rest-client`, and that anchor exists on NEITHER page: the clients/rest golden is a 9-line stub carrying no ids AT ALL, so the link is broken in the golden too -- it lands the reader at the top of a stub. The correct target is `#removing-entries-from-a-map`, which DOES exist on maintain-cluster/rest-api and which the stale fork events/distributed-object-events already links to correctly; the maintained page is the one that is wrong. WHY IT IS NOT SUBTRACTED AS GOLDEN BREAKAGE: clients/rest is dispositioned `redirect` -> maintain-cluster/rest-api, so the converter retargets the 7 inbound xrefs at conversion time and this link now resolves to a DIFFERENT path than the golden's. Layer 3's baseline identity is `dead-anchor-cross|{path}|{anchor}` (fidelity/lib/baseline.js problemKey), so moving the path changes the key and the known-broken golden entry no longer matches. The link is equally broken before and after -- the conversion introduced nothing -- but it now points at a better page. Accepted rather than fixed in the converter: repairing the fragment means encoding content knowledge about one specific link in migration tooling, and it would diverge from the golden's href either way, so it needs this entry regardless. UPSTREAM REPORT: change the xref on events/object-events.adoc to `xref:maintain-cluster:rest-api.adoc#removing-entries-from-a-map`. Fixing it upstream makes this entry go stale and the gate will say so.

  ~ /hazelcast/5.5/kubernetes/helm-upgrade-guide
      [upstream] L3: dead-anchor-cross /hazelcast/5.5/maintain-cluster/rolling-upgrades#hazelcast-members-compatibility-guarantees
          UPSTREAM STALE ANCHOR, relocated by our own version retarget -- deliberately, and this entry is the record. hz-docs' kubernetes/helm-upgrade-guide.adoc writes `xref:hazelcast:maintain-cluster:rolling-upgrades.adoc#hazelcast-members-compatibility-guarantees`, and that section was RENAMED at 5.5: the golden carries id="hazelcast-members-compatibility-guarantees" ("Compatibility Guarantees") at 5.0-5.4 and id="hazelcast-members-compatibility" ("Upgrade compatibility") from 5.5 on. So the fragment resolves nowhere at this version, whichever page it points at. It was ALREADY dead in the golden: the component-qualified versionless xref sent it to /hazelcast/5.7/, whose page has 0 occurrences of that id, and Layer 3 held it in the pre-existing-broken baseline rather than reporting it. sameComponentOwnVersion (converter/lib/html-to-mdx.js) now retargets it to the reader's OWN version, which MOVES the dead anchor and so surfaces it here as new. Accepted rather than guarded against, on a user decision (2026-09-02): both targets are dead, so the only real question is which page the reader lands on, and the top of their own version's rolling-upgrades page beats the top of 5.7's. Deliberately NOT fixed by dropping the unresolvable fragment -- that would go green while blunting Layer 3's dead-anchor detection, the net that caught our own dropped <span id> anchors. Fixed at 5.3 and 5.4 by the same retarget (their own page still has the old id), which is why only 5.5, 5.6 and 6.0-snapshot appear here. Goes stale -- and the gate says so -- if the source's anchor is ever corrected upstream and the pin moves.

  ~ /hazelcast/5.5/maintain-cluster/rest-api-swagger
      [harness-limit] L1: - "swagger_ui::https://github.com/hazelcast/hazelcast-mono/blob/5.5.z/docs/rest/modules/maintain-cluster/attachments/api-docs.yaml[]"
          The golden render deliberately does not load the swagger-ui block macro: antora/index-playbook.yml lists it under the extensions it defers, because fetching the specs needs a token for the PRIVATE product repos. So Asciidoctor leaves `swagger_ui::…[]` as literal paragraph text, and the golden for this page is one line of unrendered macro source — NOT what production serves, which is a rendered API reference. THIS HALF is the golden's macro text, which we deliberately do not reproduce. Our side renders the reference in the browser (Redoc behind BrowserOnly — the spec arrives from an async fetch and the theme is resolved from the page's live CSS), so the server-rendered HTML the comparator reads carries only the "Loading API reference…" placeholder. Neither side is the page a reader sees, so Layer 1 has no reference to compare against here rather than a conversion gap to report. Checked in the browser instead: the operations render, each heading carries a hash-link, and a deep link scrolls to the right operation. This entry goes stale — and the gate says so — if the golden ever starts rendering the macro (i.e. the extension is enabled with a token).

  ~ /hazelcast/5.5/pipelines/map-join
      [harness-limit] L3: dead-link /assets/files/nasdaqlisted-b0da91cd767daa30b2410cebdf5bf247.txt
          HARNESS LIMIT, not a conversion defect: Layer 3 resolves a link path against the ANTORA MANIFEST, which records published Antora resources and knows nothing of webpack's output, so a content-hashed asset URL can never resolve there.

HOW THE LINK BECAME A HASHED ASSET. nasdaqlisted.txt is the corpus's ONLY Antora attachment (hz-docs docs/modules/pipelines/attachments/, 195 KB), a data file this tutorial tells the reader to download. Antora publishes it and the manifest records it as `attachment$nasdaqlisted.txt`, so the production URL /hazelcast/<seg>/pipelines/_attachments/nasdaqlisted.txt IS a resolvable manifest resource -- which is why this page was Layer-3 clean before. Attachments are a different resource family from images, so extract-images.js never placed the file and the converter has no attachment path: it was silently dropped and 404ing on all 6 versions. It is now placed under static/ at that production URL, and Docusaurus resolves an ABSOLUTE asset path against every staticDirs entry (mdx-loader lib/remark/transformLinks/index.js, getLocalFileAbsolutePath): finding the file, it rewrites the link to a webpack require, so the built page carries /assets/files/nasdaqlisted-<hash>.txt -- one shared asset for all 6 versions, since identical bytes hash identically.

WHY THIS IS SAFE TO ACCEPT. That URL can only exist as the result of a require that FAILS THE BUILD if the file is missing, so the link is verified more strictly than the manifest could verify it. Note the inversion being corrected here: while the file was missing, the page carried the production URL, the manifest resolved it, and Layer 3 was GREEN on a link that genuinely 404'd for every reader. It is now red on a link that works. Accepting the hashed URL is also consistent with the standing decision that co-located image URLs are not production-preserved (webpack hashes them) -- an attachment is the same class of asset, and co-locating this one instead of using static/ would produce the SAME hashed URL and the same six entries.

The static/ copies stay so Antora's production URL keeps serving for inbound links and bookmarks; the pages themselves use the hashed asset.

GOES STALE, and the gate says so, if: the link is switched to Docusaurus's `pathname://` prefix to opt out of asset processing (the production URL returns, and these entries are no longer matched); or a Docusaurus upgrade changes the content-hash format, which moves the hash in the key so the gate re-flags it and a human re-checks -- deny-by-default working as intended rather than a maintenance burden.

      [upstream] L3: dead-anchor-cross /hazelcast/5.6/maintain-cluster/rest-api#removing-entries-from-a-map-for-rest-client
          UPSTREAM CONTENT BUG in hz-docs, already recorded in orphancheck/allowlist.json under hazelcast:events/distributed-object-events. events/object-events deep-links to `clients:rest#removing-entries-from-a-map-for-rest-client`, and that anchor exists on NEITHER page: the clients/rest golden is a 9-line stub carrying no ids AT ALL, so the link is broken in the golden too -- it lands the reader at the top of a stub. The correct target is `#removing-entries-from-a-map`, which DOES exist on maintain-cluster/rest-api and which the stale fork events/distributed-object-events already links to correctly; the maintained page is the one that is wrong. WHY IT IS NOT SUBTRACTED AS GOLDEN BREAKAGE: clients/rest is dispositioned `redirect` -> maintain-cluster/rest-api, so the converter retargets the 7 inbound xrefs at conversion time and this link now resolves to a DIFFERENT path than the golden's. Layer 3's baseline identity is `dead-anchor-cross|{path}|{anchor}` (fidelity/lib/baseline.js problemKey), so moving the path changes the key and the known-broken golden entry no longer matches. The link is equally broken before and after -- the conversion introduced nothing -- but it now points at a better page. Accepted rather than fixed in the converter: repairing the fragment means encoding content knowledge about one specific link in migration tooling, and it would diverge from the golden's href either way, so it needs this entry regardless. UPSTREAM REPORT: change the xref on events/object-events.adoc to `xref:maintain-cluster:rest-api.adoc#removing-entries-from-a-map`. Fixing it upstream makes this entry go stale and the gate will say so.

  ~ /hazelcast/5.6/kubernetes/helm-upgrade-guide
      [upstream] L3: dead-anchor-cross /hazelcast/5.6/maintain-cluster/rolling-upgrades#hazelcast-members-compatibility-guarantees
          UPSTREAM STALE ANCHOR, relocated by our own version retarget -- deliberately, and this entry is the record. hz-docs' kubernetes/helm-upgrade-guide.adoc writes `xref:hazelcast:maintain-cluster:rolling-upgrades.adoc#hazelcast-members-compatibility-guarantees`, and that section was RENAMED at 5.5: the golden carries id="hazelcast-members-compatibility-guarantees" ("Compatibility Guarantees") at 5.0-5.4 and id="hazelcast-members-compatibility" ("Upgrade compatibility") from 5.5 on. So the fragment resolves nowhere at this version, whichever page it points at. It was ALREADY dead in the golden: the component-qualified versionless xref sent it to /hazelcast/5.7/, whose page has 0 occurrences of that id, and Layer 3 held it in the pre-existing-broken baseline rather than reporting it. sameComponentOwnVersion (converter/lib/html-to-mdx.js) now retargets it to the reader's OWN version, which MOVES the dead anchor and so surfaces it here as new. Accepted rather than guarded against, on a user decision (2026-09-02): both targets are dead, so the only real question is which page the reader lands on, and the top of their own version's rolling-upgrades page beats the top of 5.7's. Deliberately NOT fixed by dropping the unresolvable fragment -- that would go green while blunting Layer 3's dead-anchor detection, the net that caught our own dropped <span id> anchors. Fixed at 5.3 and 5.4 by the same retarget (their own page still has the old id), which is why only 5.5, 5.6 and 6.0-snapshot appear here. Goes stale -- and the gate says so -- if the source's anchor is ever corrected upstream and the pin moves.

  ~ /hazelcast/5.6/maintain-cluster/rest-api-swagger
      [harness-limit] L1: - "swagger_ui::https://github.com/hazelcast/hazelcast-mono/blob/5.6.z/docs/rest/modules/maintain-cluster/attachments/api-docs.yaml[]"
          The golden render deliberately does not load the swagger-ui block macro: antora/index-playbook.yml lists it under the extensions it defers, because fetching the specs needs a token for the PRIVATE product repos. So Asciidoctor leaves `swagger_ui::…[]` as literal paragraph text, and the golden for this page is one line of unrendered macro source — NOT what production serves, which is a rendered API reference. THIS HALF is the golden's macro text, which we deliberately do not reproduce. Our side renders the reference in the browser (Redoc behind BrowserOnly — the spec arrives from an async fetch and the theme is resolved from the page's live CSS), so the server-rendered HTML the comparator reads carries only the "Loading API reference…" placeholder. Neither side is the page a reader sees, so Layer 1 has no reference to compare against here rather than a conversion gap to report. Checked in the browser instead: the operations render, each heading carries a hash-link, and a deep link scrolls to the right operation. This entry goes stale — and the gate says so — if the golden ever starts rendering the macro (i.e. the extension is enabled with a token).

  ~ /hazelcast/5.6/pipelines/map-join
      [harness-limit] L3: dead-link /assets/files/nasdaqlisted-b0da91cd767daa30b2410cebdf5bf247.txt
          HARNESS LIMIT, not a conversion defect: Layer 3 resolves a link path against the ANTORA MANIFEST, which records published Antora resources and knows nothing of webpack's output, so a content-hashed asset URL can never resolve there.

HOW THE LINK BECAME A HASHED ASSET. nasdaqlisted.txt is the corpus's ONLY Antora attachment (hz-docs docs/modules/pipelines/attachments/, 195 KB), a data file this tutorial tells the reader to download. Antora publishes it and the manifest records it as `attachment$nasdaqlisted.txt`, so the production URL /hazelcast/<seg>/pipelines/_attachments/nasdaqlisted.txt IS a resolvable manifest resource -- which is why this page was Layer-3 clean before. Attachments are a different resource family from images, so extract-images.js never placed the file and the converter has no attachment path: it was silently dropped and 404ing on all 6 versions. It is now placed under static/ at that production URL, and Docusaurus resolves an ABSOLUTE asset path against every staticDirs entry (mdx-loader lib/remark/transformLinks/index.js, getLocalFileAbsolutePath): finding the file, it rewrites the link to a webpack require, so the built page carries /assets/files/nasdaqlisted-<hash>.txt -- one shared asset for all 6 versions, since identical bytes hash identically.

WHY THIS IS SAFE TO ACCEPT. That URL can only exist as the result of a require that FAILS THE BUILD if the file is missing, so the link is verified more strictly than the manifest could verify it. Note the inversion being corrected here: while the file was missing, the page carried the production URL, the manifest resolved it, and Layer 3 was GREEN on a link that genuinely 404'd for every reader. It is now red on a link that works. Accepting the hashed URL is also consistent with the standing decision that co-located image URLs are not production-preserved (webpack hashes them) -- an attachment is the same class of asset, and co-locating this one instead of using static/ would produce the SAME hashed URL and the same six entries.

The static/ copies stay so Antora's production URL keeps serving for inbound links and bookmarks; the pages themselves use the hashed asset.

GOES STALE, and the gate says so, if: the link is switched to Docusaurus's `pathname://` prefix to opt out of asset processing (the production URL returns, and these entries are no longer matched); or a Docusaurus upgrade changes the content-hash format, which moves the hash in the key so the gate re-flags it and a human re-checks -- deny-by-default working as intended rather than a maintenance burden.

  ~ /hazelcast/5.7/events/object-events
      [upstream] L3: dead-anchor-cross /hazelcast/5.7/maintain-cluster/rest-api#removing-entries-from-a-map-for-rest-client
          UPSTREAM CONTENT BUG in hz-docs, already recorded in orphancheck/allowlist.json under hazelcast:events/distributed-object-events. events/object-events deep-links to `clients:rest#removing-entries-from-a-map-for-rest-client`, and that anchor exists on NEITHER page: the clients/rest golden is a 9-line stub carrying no ids AT ALL, so the link is broken in the golden too -- it lands the reader at the top of a stub. The correct target is `#removing-entries-from-a-map`, which DOES exist on maintain-cluster/rest-api and which the stale fork events/distributed-object-events already links to correctly; the maintained page is the one that is wrong. WHY IT IS NOT SUBTRACTED AS GOLDEN BREAKAGE: clients/rest is dispositioned `redirect` -> maintain-cluster/rest-api, so the converter retargets the 7 inbound xrefs at conversion time and this link now resolves to a DIFFERENT path than the golden's. Layer 3's baseline identity is `dead-anchor-cross|{path}|{anchor}` (fidelity/lib/baseline.js problemKey), so moving the path changes the key and the known-broken golden entry no longer matches. The link is equally broken before and after -- the conversion introduced nothing -- but it now points at a better page. Accepted rather than fixed in the converter: repairing the fragment means encoding content knowledge about one specific link in migration tooling, and it would diverge from the golden's href either way, so it needs this entry regardless. UPSTREAM REPORT: change the xref on events/object-events.adoc to `xref:maintain-cluster:rest-api.adoc#removing-entries-from-a-map`. Fixing it upstream makes this entry go stale and the gate will say so.

  ~ /hazelcast/5.7/maintain-cluster/rest-api-swagger
      [harness-limit] L1: - "swagger_ui::https://github.com/hazelcast/hazelcast-mono/blob/master/docs/rest/modules/maintain-cluster/attachments/api-docs.yaml[]"
          The golden render deliberately does not load the swagger-ui block macro: antora/index-playbook.yml lists it under the extensions it defers, because fetching the specs needs a token for the PRIVATE product repos. So Asciidoctor leaves `swagger_ui::…[]` as literal paragraph text, and the golden for this page is one line of unrendered macro source — NOT what production serves, which is a rendered API reference. THIS HALF is the golden's macro text, which we deliberately do not reproduce. Our side renders the reference in the browser (Redoc behind BrowserOnly — the spec arrives from an async fetch and the theme is resolved from the page's live CSS), so the server-rendered HTML the comparator reads carries only the "Loading API reference…" placeholder. Neither side is the page a reader sees, so Layer 1 has no reference to compare against here rather than a conversion gap to report. Checked in the browser instead: the operations render, each heading carries a hash-link, and a deep link scrolls to the right operation. This entry goes stale — and the gate says so — if the golden ever starts rendering the macro (i.e. the extension is enabled with a token).

  ~ /hazelcast/5.7/pipelines/map-join
      [harness-limit] L3: dead-link /assets/files/nasdaqlisted-b0da91cd767daa30b2410cebdf5bf247.txt
          HARNESS LIMIT, not a conversion defect: Layer 3 resolves a link path against the ANTORA MANIFEST, which records published Antora resources and knows nothing of webpack's output, so a content-hashed asset URL can never resolve there.

HOW THE LINK BECAME A HASHED ASSET. nasdaqlisted.txt is the corpus's ONLY Antora attachment (hz-docs docs/modules/pipelines/attachments/, 195 KB), a data file this tutorial tells the reader to download. Antora publishes it and the manifest records it as `attachment$nasdaqlisted.txt`, so the production URL /hazelcast/<seg>/pipelines/_attachments/nasdaqlisted.txt IS a resolvable manifest resource -- which is why this page was Layer-3 clean before. Attachments are a different resource family from images, so extract-images.js never placed the file and the converter has no attachment path: it was silently dropped and 404ing on all 6 versions. It is now placed under static/ at that production URL, and Docusaurus resolves an ABSOLUTE asset path against every staticDirs entry (mdx-loader lib/remark/transformLinks/index.js, getLocalFileAbsolutePath): finding the file, it rewrites the link to a webpack require, so the built page carries /assets/files/nasdaqlisted-<hash>.txt -- one shared asset for all 6 versions, since identical bytes hash identically.

WHY THIS IS SAFE TO ACCEPT. That URL can only exist as the result of a require that FAILS THE BUILD if the file is missing, so the link is verified more strictly than the manifest could verify it. Note the inversion being corrected here: while the file was missing, the page carried the production URL, the manifest resolved it, and Layer 3 was GREEN on a link that genuinely 404'd for every reader. It is now red on a link that works. Accepting the hashed URL is also consistent with the standing decision that co-located image URLs are not production-preserved (webpack hashes them) -- an attachment is the same class of asset, and co-locating this one instead of using static/ would produce the SAME hashed URL and the same six entries.

The static/ copies stay so Antora's production URL keeps serving for inbound links and bookmarks; the pages themselves use the hashed asset.

GOES STALE, and the gate says so, if: the link is switched to Docusaurus's `pathname://` prefix to opt out of asset processing (the production URL returns, and these entries are no longer matched); or a Docusaurus upgrade changes the content-hash format, which moves the hash in the key so the gate re-flags it and a human re-checks -- deny-by-default working as intended rather than a maintenance burden.

      [harness-limit] L3: dead-link /assets/files/nasdaqlisted-b0da91cd767daa30b2410cebdf5bf247.txt
          HARNESS LIMIT, not a conversion defect: Layer 3 resolves a link path against the ANTORA MANIFEST, which records published Antora resources and knows nothing of webpack's output, so a content-hashed asset URL can never resolve there.

HOW THE LINK BECAME A HASHED ASSET. nasdaqlisted.txt is the corpus's ONLY Antora attachment (hz-docs docs/modules/pipelines/attachments/, 195 KB), a data file this tutorial tells the reader to download. Antora publishes it and the manifest records it as `attachment$nasdaqlisted.txt`, so the production URL /hazelcast/<seg>/pipelines/_attachments/nasdaqlisted.txt IS a resolvable manifest resource -- which is why this page was Layer-3 clean before. Attachments are a different resource family from images, so extract-images.js never placed the file and the converter has no attachment path: it was silently dropped and 404ing on all 6 versions. It is now placed under static/ at that production URL, and Docusaurus resolves an ABSOLUTE asset path against every staticDirs entry (mdx-loader lib/remark/transformLinks/index.js, getLocalFileAbsolutePath): finding the file, it rewrites the link to a webpack require, so the built page carries /assets/files/nasdaqlisted-<hash>.txt -- one shared asset for all 6 versions, since identical bytes hash identically.

WHY THIS IS SAFE TO ACCEPT. That URL can only exist as the result of a require that FAILS THE BUILD if the file is missing, so the link is verified more strictly than the manifest could verify it. Note the inversion being corrected here: while the file was missing, the page carried the production URL, the manifest resolved it, and Layer 3 was GREEN on a link that genuinely 404'd for every reader. It is now red on a link that works. Accepting the hashed URL is also consistent with the standing decision that co-located image URLs are not production-preserved (webpack hashes them) -- an attachment is the same class of asset, and co-locating this one instead of using static/ would produce the SAME hashed URL and the same six entries.

The static/ copies stay so Antora's production URL keeps serving for inbound links and bookmarks; the pages themselves use the hashed asset.

GOES STALE, and the gate says so, if: the link is switched to Docusaurus's `pathname://` prefix to opt out of asset processing (the production URL returns, and these entries are no longer matched); or a Docusaurus upgrade changes the content-hash format, which moves the hash in the key so the gate re-flags it and a human re-checks -- deny-by-default working as intended rather than a maintenance burden.

  ~ /management-center/5.10/clusters/create-filter-lists-rest
      [upstream] L2: links golden=10 converted=12
          Layer 2 counts NAVIGABLE links only: fidelity/lib/structure.js excludes any `<a href>` containing `<` or `>`, since such an href can never be a valid destination. The golden's two deep links into the client-filtering API page are exactly that — `#/paths/<sub>1rest</sub>1clusters<sub>1%7Bcluster%7D</sub>1clientfiltering~1lists/post` — because Asciidoctor applied its SUBSCRIPT substitution to the `~` runs of the JSON pointer the source authored (create-filter-lists-rest.adoc:18 and :37). They are dead on the live site today for that reason, and excluded from the golden's count of 10. The converter repairs them (converter/lib/links.js repairApiDeepLink) and retargets them to the anchor the page publishes, so ours are real links and are counted — hence 12. The +2 IS the repair. UPSTREAM: the source needs those `~` runs protected from substitution; fixing it there makes the golden's links navigable, both counts 12, and this entry stale.
      [harness-limit] L3: dead-anchor-cross /management-center/5.10/integrate/client-filtering#post-rest-clusters-cluster-clientfiltering-lists
          Layer 3 resolves a cross-page anchor against the GOLDEN anchor index, and an API page's golden is a single line of unrendered macro text with no ids on it at all (see the L1 entries for this page's target, and why the golden is blank). So NO anchor into an API page can resolve there, whatever it is — the index structurally cannot contain an operation id, because the operations only exist once the spec is rendered. The anchor itself is real and is the one the page publishes: plugins/openapi-specs reads the operations off the staged spec and src/components/ApiReference registers them with collectAnchor during SSR, so Docusaurus's own onBrokenAnchors validates it and no longer reports this link (103 -> 99 page blocks). Verified in the browser: the link scrolls to the matching operation heading.

      [wont-fix] L1: - "If a feature in the documentation requires an Enterprise license, it is labelled Enterprise."
          DELIBERATE DIVERGENCE, decided by Miranda 2026-08-27: the inline enterprise badge has ONE label site-wide (src/components/Badge), and the two products' sources disagree about what it says. Platform writes the attribute — [.enterprise]*{enterprise-product-name}* — which resolves to "Enterprise Edition" on 37 pages; MC writes the literal "Enterprise" on this one page only, in a sentence that DESCRIBES the badge.   ~ /management-center/5.12-snapshot/clusters/create-filter-lists-rest
      [upstream] L2: links golden=10 converted=12
          Layer 2 counts NAVIGABLE links only: fidelity/lib/structure.js excludes any `<a href>` containing `<` or `>`, since such an href can never be a valid destination. The golden's two deep links into the client-filtering API page are exactly that — `#/paths/<sub>1rest</sub>1clusters<sub>1%7Bcluster%7D</sub>1clientfiltering~1lists/post` — because Asciidoctor applied its SUBSCRIPT substitution to the `~` runs of the JSON pointer the source authored (create-filter-lists-rest.adoc:18 and :37). They are dead on the live site today for that reason, and excluded from the golden's count of 10. The converter repairs them (converter/lib/links.js repairApiDeepLink) and retargets them to the anchor the page publishes, so ours are real links and are counted — hence 12. The +2 IS the repair. UPSTREAM: the source needs those `~` runs protected from substitution; fixing it there makes the golden's links navigable, both counts 12, and this entry stale.
      [harness-limit] L3: dead-anchor-cross /management-center/5.12-snapshot/integrate/client-filtering#post-rest-clusters-cluster-clientfiltering-lists
          Layer 3 resolves a cross-page anchor against the GOLDEN anchor index, and an API page's golden is a single line of unrendered macro text with no ids on it at all (see the L1 entries for this page's target, and why the golden is blank). So NO anchor into an API page can resolve there, whatever it is — the index structurally cannot contain an operation id, because the operations only exist once the spec is rendered. The anchor itself is real and is the one the page publishes: plugins/openapi-specs reads the operations off the staged spec and src/components/ApiReference registers them with collectAnchor during SSR, so Docusaurus's own onBrokenAnchors validates it and no longer reports this link (103 -> 99 page blocks). Verified in the browser: the link scrolls to the matching operation heading.

      [wont-fix] L1: - "If a feature in the documentation requires an Enterprise license, it is labelled Enterprise."
          DELIBERATE DIVERGENCE, decided by Miranda 2026-08-27: the inline enterprise badge has ONE label site-wide (src/components/Badge), and the two products' sources disagree about what it says. Platform writes the attribute — [.enterprise]*{enterprise-product-name}* — which resolves to "Enterprise Edition" on 37 pages; MC writes the literal "Enterprise" on this one page only, in a sentence that DESCRIBES the badge. Both components' enterprise-product-name is "Enterprise Edition", so MC's literal is a source inconsistency, not a different value. The registry label is now "Enterprise Edition", which makes 37 Platform pages fidelity-clean and makes this sentence agree with the badge rendered beside it — so our output is more correct than the golden. THIS HALF is the golden's "labelled Enterprise", which we deliberately do not reproduce. Goes stale — and the gate will say so — if the source is fixed to use {enterprise-product-name} here too.

  ~ /operator/5.17/image-registry
      [upstream] L1: - "Hazelcast does not publish Red Hat images for snapshot versions."
          UPSTREAM SOURCE BUG in hazelcast-platform-operator-docs `docs/antora.yml` on the RELEASE branches: v/5.17, v/5.18 and v/5.19 all declare `snapshot: true`, which belongs only on main and was copied over when those branches were cut (v/5.16 is clean, which is exactly why 5.16 does not show this diff). image-registry.adoc wraps this note in `ifdef::snapshot[]`, so Antora renders it on those released versions — the LIVE 5.18 and 5.17 pages currently tell readers there are no Red Hat images for "snapshot versions". Our <IfSnapshot> evaluates false for a released version and suppresses it (verified in the built output: present only on latest-snapshot). Fix = delete `snapshot: true` from v/5.17, v/5.18 and v/5.19 antora.yml; the entry then goes stale and the gate will say so.

          The Layer-2 counterpart of the image-registry diff above: the golden has two notes on the page because the release branch wrongly defines `snapshot`, ours has one because <IfSnapshot> correctly suppresses the snapshot-only note for a released version. Same single root cause — `snapshot: true` in v/5.17, v/5.18 and v/5.19 antora.yml.

  ~ /privacy/
      [harness-limit] L1: + "Loading the privacy policy..."
          NEITHER SIDE IS THE PAGE A READER SEES, so Layer 1 has no reference to compare against here rather than a conversion gap to report -- the same situation as the Swagger pages, and accepted the same way. The policy text lives in NO repo: the Antora source (hazelcast-docs privacy/modules/ROOT/pages/index.adoc) is four lines, a title and `:page-layout: privacy`, and all content is injected client-side by the UI layout (hazelcast-docs-ui privacy.hbs) from https://hazelcast.com/wp-json/wp/v2/pages?slug=privacy. So the GOLDEN for this page is a 1-BYTE EMPTY FILE, by construction and not by breakage. src/pages/privacy.tsx replicates that fetch deliberately, so Legal's edits reach the page without a docs rebuild; the server-rendered HTML therefore carries exactly one string, the loading placeholder. THIS HALF is our placeholder, which the golden has no counterpart for. It is TRANSIENT and provably so from the source: `state` starts 'loading' and the effect always resolves to 'ready' or 'error'. RENDERED CHECK: done previously via the same-origin fixture route (save the WP payload into static/ and fetch it same-origin) -- that pass is what produced the measured findings recorded in the docusaurus-privacy-page note, including the payload's two unscoped <style> blocks and its SiteOrigin panel-grid nesting. It CANNOT be re-checked from a dev server or a preview, because the WP API sends `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: https://docs.hazelcast.com` -- a single pinned origin -- so from any non-production origin the fetch fails and the page shows its error state. Whoever owns the WordPress config must add any new origin before cutover. NOT RELATED TO ANY VERSION WORK: this diff appeared when privacy.tsx first emitted build/privacy/index.html (2026-09-01), because the fidelity harness is GOLDEN-DRIVEN -- goldenPages() walks every golden page and compares it wherever a converted file exists at the matching path, regardless of migration/lib/layout.js NAV_SOURCES. `/home/` has the same 1-byte golden and escapes only because nothing emits at /home/ (the home page is served at /), so it pairs with nothing and counts as 'not yet converted'. There is no empty-golden guard in the harness; adding one was considered and declined in favour of this entry, which keeps the page visible in the report. Goes stale -- and the gate says so -- if the golden ever starts carrying the policy text (i.e. the content stops being a runtime fetch).

The only real loss is the "Figure N." counter and a "Screenshot - " prefix.
